#6887. The Top Five Brands to Know This Winter

With sharp tailoring and standout knitwear, MR PORTER offers a variety of collections for dressing well in the cold.

Winter cold gives you a reason to wear the clothes that make getting dressed interesting: a proper wool coat, a substantial sweater, trousers with some weight to them, and boots built for more than walking from the car to the restaurant. Then the holiday parties start, and your usual uniform needs an upgrade. A good wardrobe should handle all of it without requiring you to develop a different personality for every occasion. And because you’re wearing more of your clothes at once, the details matter. Fit, fabric, and proportion can make the difference between looking put together and looking like you got dressed in the dark.

MR PORTER is a great place to start building that versatile wardrobe. Its assortment brings together brands with a clear understanding of what each does well, from Tom Ford’s sharp tailoring to Brunello Cucinelli’s easy sophistication and Begg & Co’s expertise in knitwear and accessories. Yoke offers a more experimental take on everyday dressing, while Mr P focuses on the everyday staples that make getting dressed easier. The common threads are thoughtful design, good materials, and construction that holds up to repeated wear.

We’ve picked two pieces from each brand that make a compelling case for adding them to your cold-weather wardrobe.

Tom Ford launched his namesake label in 2005 after revitalizing Gucci throughout the 1990s with a sexier, more provocative take on luxury. The brand is known for sharp tailoring, sleek silhouettes, and a glamorous sensibility that extends from eveningwear to leather goods and footwear. Today, designer Haider Ackermann leads its creative direction, carrying that legacy forward with his own modern vision.

The designer’s tailoring has always favored a sharp silhouette, and these brown wool tuxedo pants put a more relaxed spin on formalwear. Double pleats give the straight legs room to move, while pressed creases keep the shape crisp. Wear them with a black dinner jacket for a holiday party that calls for something beyond the usual uniform, or pair them with a fine-gauge black sweater and polished loafers for a winter dinner.

A good winter night-out look starts from the ground up. Tom Ford’s Chelsea boots bring a little rock-and-roll swagger to the occasion, with glossy, lizard-effect leather, sharply pointed toes, and stacked heels that give the classic slip-on silhouette a Western edge. They’re the sort of boots that can turn a dark suit into a statement or give black jeans and a wool coat some much-needed attitude. Wear them to a holiday party, a late dinner, or anywhere your sneakers might send the wrong message.

Founded in Umbria in 1978, Brunello Cucinelli began with a simple idea: make cashmere sweaters for women in colors that felt fresh and modern. The Italian label has since expanded into a full menswear and womenswear collection, becoming known for luxurious knitwear, fine tailoring, and an easy approach to dressing well. Its clothes combine traditional Italian craftsmanship with a relaxed sophistication that works just as well for everyday wear as it does for more formal occasions.

Brunello Cucinelli has built a following around luxurious fabrics and Italian tailoring that manage to feel relaxed. This wool-felt overshirt puts that approach to work in a more casual silhouette, with flap chest pockets, silver-tone snap fastenings, and a curved hem. The black wool is substantial enough to take the edge off a cold city morning, while the clean design makes it a smart alternative to a traditional jacket. Wear it over a fine-knit sweater with wool trousers for the office, then head straight to dinner.

Brunello Cucinelli’s cashmere expertise is on full display in this black ribbed zip-up sweater, knitted in Italy with a relaxed fit and a stand collar that keeps the cold off your neck. The two-way zipper lets you adjust the coverage, whether you’re layering it under a wool overcoat for a weekend spent exploring the city or wearing it on its own during a long flight. It’s a clever way to make travel-day comfort feel considerably more considered.

Mr P. has an advantage most menswear labels don’t: it was developed by the people at MR PORTER who spend their days studying what makes clothes worth buying. Launched in 2017, the in-house brand focuses on wardrobe staples, from knitwear and denim to outerwear, with an emphasis on quality materials and details that make sense in everyday life. The idea is to get the fundamentals right, then give men plenty of ways to wear them.

A polo shirt can do more than a button-down when the occasion calls for looking presentable without getting too dressed up. Mr P.’s long-sleeved version is knitted in Italy from 75 percent merino wool and 25 percent silk, giving the fine-gauge fabric a soft feel and subtle sheen. An open collar and ribbed trims keep the design streamlined. A smart alternative to another crewneck sweater, this can be worn with flannel trousers for a holiday lunch or under a tailored coat when you’re meeting friends for a winter matinee.

Some winter days require a coat that does more than keep you warm. Mr P.’s green shearling number has plenty of personality, with a suede exterior, plush lining, and a broad collar that frames the face. The loose fit leaves room for a chunky sweater underneath, while the button front and slanted pockets keep things practical. Wear it with dark denim and sturdy boots for a Saturday spent browsing record shops, grabbing lunch, or walking home the long way. You might even forget how cold it is.

Founded by designer Norio Terada in Tokyo in 2018, Yoke takes its name from the garment panel that joins pieces of fabric together. That idea of connection carries through the label’s approach to design, which draws on art and reworks familiar menswear with unexpected proportions, interesting textures, and subtle twists. The result is clothing that feels relaxed and easy to wear, with enough edge to keep a wardrobe of everyday staples from looking predictable.

Yoke approaches everyday clothing with a Japanese design sensibility that puts proportion and considered details at the forefront. These wide-leg jeans show how much a change in silhouette can do. Cut from black cotton denim, they have a roomy leg and a classic five-pocket design, with enough volume to balance a chunky knit or a boxy winter coat. Wear them with a pair of leather sneakers for an afternoon antique hunting or checking out a gallery.

Made in Japan from a blend of 85 percent wool and 15 percent cashmere, this gray felt overcoat has a modern silhouette withdropped shoulders, wide notch lapels, and an oversized double-breasted cut that leaves plenty of room for winter layers. The fabric feels substantial without weighing you down, making it the perfect choice for days when you’re walking across town rather than hopping between cabs. Throw it over a chunky sweater and wide-leg jeans for a look that feels polished without being overly formal.

Begg & Co’s story begins in Paisley, Scotland, in 1866, when Alex Begg established a textile business that would become known for its scarves and cashmere. Today, the brand makes knitwear and accessories at mills in Ayr and Hawick, combining traditional Scottish textile techniques with modern design. Its cashmere collection ranges from classic scarves to substantial sweaters, with texture and color often doing the work that logos do elsewhere.

Begg & Co has been making textiles in Scotland since the 19th century, and its experience shows in the details of this navy scarf. The Bernard is knitted from a blend of 56 percent cashmere and 44 percent lambswool, with a repeating cable pattern that gives it texture and wide ribbed ends that keep the design clean. At nearly 7 feet long, it can be wrapped snugly around your neck on a windy morning or left loose over a wool coat when you want a little more warmth on the way to a weekend lunch.

Begg & Co’s Surfer cardigan is knitted in Scotland from pure cashmere, with blended yarns that give the ribbed surface subtle tonal variation. Horn buttons, welt pockets, and a shawl collar lend it a classic look, while the substantial knit makes it a practical alternative to a lightweight jacket. Wear it over a T-shirt with relaxed trousers for a slow Sunday morning at home, then keep it on when you head out for coffee or an impromptu lunch. It’s an easy way to look put together without getting dressed up.

Expert tip: I found early MacBook deals across several of Apple’s most popular models, including $300 off the 2026 MacBook Pro and $200 off the 13-inch and 15-inch 2026 MacBook Air. That said, none of these laptops have hit record-low prices quite yet, so you stand to save more money if you wait.

Expert tip: Last but not least, I spotted some gems in the accessories department. At $23 for one or $70 for a four-pack, the AirTag is on sale for its lowest price ever (and makes for a great holiday gift). Plus, you can save over 20% on the Apple Pencil, and USB-C charging cable.

#6884. Jacquard Is Having a Moment—Here’s How to Wear It Without Looking Overdone

Statement blazers, muted florals, and metallic touches make jacquard feel modern.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Jacquard is having a moment, and it’s easy to see why. Woven rather than printed, these richly patterned fabrics carry their designs in the very structure of the cloth: florals that rise in subtle relief, geometric motifs that catch the light, tone-on-tone brocades that shimmer when you move. It’s a fabric with the soul of couture and, increasingly, the versatility of everyday dressing.

But a textile this expressive comes with a question every fashion lover eventually asks: how do you wear it without looking like you’ve raided the sofa upholstery?

The answer, as it turns out, is balance. Jacquard works best when it’s allowed to be the star, so the rest of your outfit should play a supporting role. Think a statement blazer paired with clean white tees and straight-leg denim, or a sculptural jacquard miniskirt grounded by a fitted knit and sleek ankle boots. For evenings, a jacquard slip dress needs little more than a sharp jacket and a good pair of heels. Metallic threads and deep jewel tones lean glamorous, while soft neutrals and muted palettes bring a quiet, modern elegance to workwear and weekend looks alike.

Ahead, we’ve gathered some street style inspo that proves jacquard belongs far beyond the red carpet.

Anchored by black leggings and sleek tall boots, this structured jacquard jacket commands the spotlight.

Paired with subtly ruffled denim and a few modern touches, a fitted jacquard blazer brings a hint of Versailles to the street.

Floral jacquard trousers in muted tones prove far more wearable than expected. We love how she paired them with a coordinating knit that echoes the palette without matching the print.

An all-black outfit gets a sophisticated, edgy upgrade with a black and gold jacquard blazer. We love how the mix of sparkly and sculptural jewelry mirrors the metallic shimmer and completes the look.

This skirt and jacket set is a perfect fit for an event or social gathering. The bronze heels provide a striking contrast, while the tiny handbag blends seamlessly into the look.

The contrast of a golden jacquard blazer with an hourglass silhouette against an all-black ensemble proves that a single statement piece can transform an outfit, adding warmth, shine, and a flattering shape without any extra effort.

#6881. 15 Paint Colors HGTV Stars Swear By for a Home That Looks Expensive

From sea blue to crisp white, these designer favorites are timeless.

Everyone knows that a quick coat of paint is the fastest way to transform a room. It’s a favorite trick among professional designers and DIYers alike, but choosing the perfect shade isn't always easy. With hundreds of colors to choose from, finding “the one” can quickly become overwhelming. If you’re feeling a little stuck, don’t fret: HGTV’s biggest stars make these tough decisions every day.

Whether they’re renovating an outdated home or putting the finishing touches on a dream build, HGTV personalities are constantly selecting paint colors that make a space feel fresh, spacious, inviting, and beautifully designed. Over the years, they’ve crowned a select few as go-to favorites that are foolproof and versatile enough to work in almost any room. When every renovation comes with deadlines and budgets, dependable paint colors become invaluable.

To help narrow your search, we asked some of our favorite HGTV stars to reveal the paint colors they reach for most often. Their picks include everything from versatile neutrals to powdery pastels that work across a variety of homes and aesthetics. Additional copy by Alyssa Khan.

“We love muted colors because they’re playful, but not over-the-top. Behr Mocha Ice is a perfect example of that balance—it brings the personality you need to take a space from basic and bland to warm and interesting. Try it in a bedroom, dining room, or anywhere that’s missing that ‘wow’ factor.”—Drew Scott, Property Brothers

“This beautiful blue is timeless and versatile. It’s calming, but not boring. Even though Behr's Dragonfly makes a statement, it’s still neutral enough for many spaces, whether it’s on every wall in a bedroom or used on a specific feature like kitchen cabinets.”—Jonathan Scott, Property Brothers

“This never-boring black paint color is dramatic and impactful yet very versatile. Pair with white for a timeless aesthetic. Use it to create a dramatic accent wall or outside for your exterior (like we did) or trim for maximum curb appeal.”—Tarek El Moussa, The Flip Off

“My favorite paint color is Tricorn Black by Sherwin-Williams. It’s bold, clean, and makes every detail pop. Whether it’s a door, a wall, or literally anything else, this black brings that high-end look every single time. Never fails.”—Cole DeBoer, Down Home Fab

“I am currently loving greige, the perfect blend of gray and beige. It’s warm, soft, and timeless—a great option for homeowners who want both cozy and modern. Benjamin Moore's Revere Pewter is a classic and rich greige with warmth. Great for open-concept homes.”—Heather Rae El Moussa, The Flip Off

“What I love most about it is its confidence. Studio Green by Farrow & Ball doesn’t whisper: It commands. It’s deep, moody, and unapologetically rich, the kind of color that instantly elevates a space and gives it a point of view. In daylight, it reads as a classic, almost architectural green, grounded and refined. But at night? It transforms into something velvety and dramatic, wrapping the room in a quiet luxury that feels both intimate and powerful.”—Egypt Sherrod, Married to Real Estate andProperty Virgins

“Benjamin Moore Misty Air OC-44 is my constant when I need a bright neutral. It is a broken-in cream that’s the perfect backdrop for colorful art and upholstery.”—Erin Napier, Home Town

““My current favorite paint color is Black Fox by Sherwin-Williams. It’s the perfect mix of sophisticated and moody, with just enough warmth to make any space feel grounded and timeless. It pairs beautifully with so many other tones and textures. I love it in an office!”—Chelsea DeBoer, Down Home Fab

Magnolia Home by Joanna Gaines Juniper Tree Crafted by KILZ

“Seed & Supply was one of the first additions we made to the Silos back in 2015. Before it served as our garden shop on the grounds, it was used as an additional retail space at the original Magnolia shop, which we lovingly refer to as The Little Shop on Bosque. This year, the team had the idea to evolve the building yet again, but this time to serve as a creative workshop space for our guests. I immediately loved the idea. It has always been my hope that our guests’ experience at the Silos would inspire creativity and connection.

The whole interior of the workshop is refreshed with one color, Juniper Tree Trim, Door, and Cabinetry Paint in satin finish. I love how these nature-forward hues turned out—they feel fresh yet familiar, bold yet comforting.”—Joanna Gaines, Fixer Upper

““White Flour is the perfect soft white backdrop for every style. It accentuates simplicity or maximalist energy. The warm hue is anything but basic.”—Taniya Nayak, Battle on the Beach

“I love Farrow & Ball's Drop Cloth for walls, or for trim and cabinetry with a more true white on the wall. It is a timeless neutral that looks beautiful, particularly in historic homes. This warm neutral evokes calm tranquility without being sterile. It is the perfect first layer for any space: It sets a foundation of simplicity that can easily be layered upon with color on cabinetry, trim detail, furniture, art, and décor.”—Jenny Marrs, Fixer to Fabulous

“I keep going back to Behr White. It’s porcelain-like and a classic white without being too stark or jarring. It also has a very slight undertone of blue, which I prefer over yellow or pink undertones, as a slight hint of blue preserves more of a pure white tone.”—Galey Alix, Home in a Heartbeat With Galey Alix

“My current favorite color is Benjamin Moore Windsor Green. I just painted my bedroom this color! The walls, the doors, the windows, and the trim. It's just so deep and lovely. To me, a deep mossy green is cozy and sophisticated at the same time. It steals the show every time, which is why I went for it in my own bedroom!”—Kristina Crestin, Farmhouse Fixer

In her blog for the Laurel Mercantile, the Home Town Takeover co-host crowned Dover White her all-time favorite hue.

“My all-time favorite white, forever and always, to the point my design team just defaults to it when they aren't able to get a quick decision from me.”— Erin Napier, Home Town Takeover

Fix My Flip star, Page Turner, shared her favorite shade of white with HGTV fans. Boasting warm cream and soft beige undertones, Whisper White is the perfect airy neutral.

“I like how this shade isn’t as stark as other white paints can be.” — Page Turner, Fix My Flip

Drew used it to coat the guest bedroom ceiling in the Season 6 Canyon House in 2018. The serene shade has remained in their palette ever since.

#6880. How to Wear Velvet Pants, According to the Runways

From Burberry’s utilitarian twist to Tory Burch’s stripes, the runways make the case for wearing velvet pants every day.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

When you think of velvet, what comes to mind? For me, it has always been a cozy-chic holiday fabric. But that’s no longer the whole story. Velvet has graduated from special-occasion dressing to an everyday staple.

Designers like Tory Burch, Stella McCartney, and Burberry have shown that velvet can live alongside the rest of your wardrobe, not just next to your holiday party picks. The texture is cozy yet elevated, which is why it’s gaining more creative freedom with every season.

The easiest way to start is to treat velvet pants like any other trouser you’d reach for on repeat. Pair them with a fine-gauge knit, a crisp white shirt, or a matching tailored blazer, and let the fabric’s soft sheen do the talking. Swap in loafers or flats for the day, then switch to heels and a silk blouse for evening.

With velvet’s texture already doing the heavy lifting, accessories can stay simple and fuss-free. Wear them a few times, and you’ll realize just how versatile velvet trousers can be.

Below, get the runway-inspired edition of how to wear velvet pants this fall.

Velvet in bold jewel tones, as shown at Akris, makes more than a textural statement. For the full velvet effect, pair your trousers with a matching blazer and a coat in an equally soft, plush texture.

At Burberry, dark navy velvet pants were paired with a light beige rain jacket. The result is part sophisticated-chic, part utilitarian.

At Polo Ralph Lauren, wide-leg velvet trousers were styled with a knit layered over a collared button-up, a preppy look with a plush twist.

Consider the Ralph Lauren Collection approach: a romantic sheer ruffled blouse paired with a velvet duster coat.

Proenza Schouler’s tailored velvet suit pairs sharp attitude with a hint of playfulness.

Tory Burch proved that velvet and stripes are a perfect match. Pair velvet trousers with a simple henley tee, then add a statement accessory to finish it. A sculptural belt, dangly earrings, and oversized sunglasses give the outfit a focal point without competing with the velvet’s texture.

Let us move on up to Alaska, where, after The New York Times expressed horror at a sharp political trick that has been around since the dawn of time, the wheels of the official ratfck have begun to turn. From ABC News:

Horrors! Those of us who grew up in the Commonwealth (God save it!) find the outraged innocence of the governor—and that of the NYT—pretty damn hilarious. Steve M. of No More Mister Nice Blog, also a local lad, compared the ploy to a similar gambit in a Massachusetts governor race. As is the case with the two Dan Sullivans in Alaska, there were two Edward Kings on the ballot differing only in their middle names. It has no effect on the eventual election; the Democratic Ed King won by about 100,000 and was a terrible governor, by the way.

And if you need your shenanigans committed by historical icons, remember that, in 1946, when John F. Kennedy first ran for Congress, two candidates named Joe Russo mysteriously turned up against him in the Democratic primary. One of the Joe Russos was a Boston city councilor and a popular local politician. The other one was a janitor. Local historians long ago revealed that Janitor Joe was paid by the Kennedy campaign to run in the primary and split the Joe Russo vote. JFK won in a landslide anyway. Voters are not as dumb as Alaska Republicans say they are.

One more election item. Up in Minnesota, they’ve rousted a notorious ratfcker. From NBC News:

Oh my, James. Get that pardon payoff in order. Natalie is standing by.

Up until James of Arabia showed up, the voucher system had been perfectly adequate for Minnesotans. It allowed people in the same precinct to vote essentially by proxy for those people who otherwise could not vote because, for example, they’d just moved and don’t yet have acceptable proof of residence. At its best, it was an act of kindness, which to people like James O’Keefe is like garlic to a vampire.

And we conclude, as is our custom, in the great state of Oklahoma, whence Blog Official Ginja Sampler Friedman of the Algarve brings us a tale of local government as corn maze. From Oklahoma Watch:

These people demanded a court order, got one, and then appealed it? Does anyone here know how to play this game?

This is your democracy, America. Cherish it.

#6877. The Best Rhode Skincare Products, According to Experts

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

I’ve tried every single one of Hailey Bieber’s Rhode products, and these are my all-time favorites

The Hailey Bieber effect is real—from inspiring others to wear pimple patches on their feet to creating a chrome nail craze. If you are staying up to date with her, then you likely already know that Hailey Bieber sold her brand, Rhode, to e.l.f. Beauty for a whopping $1 billion, just a short three years after she launched the brand. Since the sale, the brand has only accelerated, creating viral product after viral product.

While a billion dollars is a lot, the sale doesn’t come as much of a surprise when you consider the popularity of the products. The formulas are fan favorites among those on TikTok, as well as among the Bazaar team. In a sea of celebrity brands, Rhode stands out, partly because Bieber enlisted the help of skincare professionals like cosmetic chemist Ron Robinson, who created his own line, BeautyStat, and New York dermatologist Dhaval Bhanusali to help formulate the products.

“My ethos behind the brand is that we want to make one of everything,” Bieber told Bazaar when the brand launched in 2022. “One really good moisturizer, one really good hydration serum, one really good lip product. I believe in curating your essentials in your closet the same way I believe in curating your essentials in your skincare.”

Rhode now has seven skincare formulas (including the latest Glazing Mist launch) and has even expanded into making creamy blushes, plush lip tints, and shaping lip liners. But the skincare offerings are still at the core of Bieber’s brand. Ahead, our favorite Rhode skin products that are worth the hype and deliver on the promise of Hailey Bieber-worthy glazed doughnut skin.

Rhode’s Glazing Milk is the product that converted me to a Rhode stan. While the milky essence category continues to grow, Rhode was one of the first Stateside brands to jump on the bandwagon.

The Glazing Milk has a lightweight yet creamy consistency. The ingredient list is like a vitamin shot for your skin, featuring a trio of ceramides, beta-glucan to calm the skin, and a blend of magnesium, zinc, and copper that protects your barrier. It’s the perfect hydration base layer for your skincare routine or can be mixed with a foundation to create a DIY skin tint.

It sounds gimmicky that this mask can depuff and sculpt the face, but the caffeine- and peptide-powered formula truly leaves skin firmer, tighter, and brighter in under 10 minutes.

The Peptide Glazing Fluid was one of Rhode’s first products, offering a glimpse into Hailey Bieber’s plumping skincare routine. While hydrating serums sometimes conjure thick, gloopy textures, this formula is more like a light gel that instantly sinks into the skin. It gently hydrates skin, leaving a subtle glow. My skin drinks up this serum, and it’s the perfect layer under makeup or as an extra layer of hydration and plumping before bed.

There’s a reason this cleanser won a Harper’s Bazaar Skincare Award, and why it’s become my go-to cleanser. With most face washes, I still feel dirt, makeup, and grime left on my face even after a thorough scrub, but the lather on this balm-to-foam cleanser is unmatched—it takes the day off in one fell swoop. Pineapple is the star of the show in this cleanser—specifically pineapple enzyme, which sloughs off dead skin—while polyglutamic acid and green tea extract hydrate.

The Peptide Lip Boost, a nourishing lip mask from Rhode, may be our favorite lip formula from the brand. It’s thicker than the Peptide Lip Treatment and feels more like a hefty balm, providing extra hydration and cushion. Plus, it leaves behind a very slight tingle, which feels more refreshing than painful and plumps the lips and softens fine lines.

Ever since Rhode launched its cooling and depuffing eye patches, they have been staples in Bazaar editors’ routines. The patches instantly smooth and depuff the under-eye area, and they don’t budge, so they are perfect for multitasking.

The brand’s latest skincare launch is like an SOS button for parched, irritated skin. A thicker, more hydrating iteration of the brand’s Barrier Restore Cream, the Barrier Butter harnesses the power of hyaluronic acid (utilizing five different molecular weights of the ingredient) and ceramides, along with nourishing, rich murumuru butter, to lock in moisture.

I love using the rich, buttery cream as the last layer of my nighttime routine, but also on any dry patches from head to toe. Hailey Bieber told us she uses the Barrier Butter everywhere—from her neck and chest to her hands and even her arms.

The lip balm that started it all, Rhode’s Peptide Lip Treatment went viral when the brand first launched, and for good reason. While the texture of this skincare-infused treatment feels more like a gloss than a traditional treatment balm, it leaves lips with a high-shine finish that also plumps courtesy of peptides. The treatment has now expanded into tinted versions and has even inspired other peptide-forward lip products, such as the Peptide Lip Shape liner.

The brand’s newest launch is a hydrating face mist designed to refresh the skin and add a glow with a single spritz. While I haven’t gotten my hands on the Glazing Mist yet, it has all the hallmarks of a great mist, including a superfine mister. The formula boasts a ceramide trio to improve the skin barrier, ectoin to keep skin hydrated throughout the day, and panthenol to soothe skin.

Many moisturizers on the market contain actives such as acids or retinol, but Rhode’s face moisturizer has zero actives—just ingredients that focus on calming, softening, and hydrating the skin. When my perioral dermatitis flares up, I reach for this lightweight yet moisturizing formula, which is filled with squalane, peptides, niacinamide, shea butter, and açaí.

While Rhode wasn’t the first to jazz up pimple patches, these 100 percent hydrocolloid patches stay in place while healing and protecting breakouts. You can also use them on blisters, as demonstrated by Bieber.

#6875. Suki Waterhouse Just Revealed a Major Update About Her Super-Private Relationship With Robert Pattinson

After years of rumors, Suki finally set the record straight.

Robert Pattinson and Zendaya have a lot in common: They’re both Earth signs, they’ve both starred in superhero movies, they both appear in three of this year’s biggest movies (The Drama, The Odyssey, and Dune: Part Three), and—most importantly—no one could figure out whether either one of them was married...until pretty recently, that is.

It’s been a minute since Robert and his longtime love, Suki Waterhouse, welcomed their first child together (and nearly three years since they got engaged), but Suki FINALLY cleared up her relationship status with Robert—and unlike Zendaya and Tom Holland, they’re not actually married!

During a new interview with the Sunday Times, Suki confirmed that she and Robert haven’t yet tied the knot and aren’t ruling out a “massive” wedding. “It would either be all or nothing,” she said of their upcoming nuptials. “If we were going to go for it, we’d have a massive party, blow it out.”

In light of this new development, I think the time has come for a handy journey through their relationship timeline, starting at the very beginning.

Robert and Suki are spotted for the first time, and the PDA is On. Another. Level. By which I mean they’re seen kissing after a showing of Mamma Mia! Here We Go Again (wow, hot) in some pics published by E! News.

“Along the way, they were very close and cuddled and kissed many times,” an eyewitness says at the time. “Robert was very affectionate to her and had his arm around her the whole way and Suki reciprocated by holding his hand and kissing it.”

Cute, but FYI: This same month, another source downplays the relationship to E! News, saying Suki and Robert are simply “casually dating.”

The Daily Mail out of nowhere, randomly reports that Robert and Suki have broken up. Which, nope! But before long, Lainey Gossip disputes the claim, pointing out that on top of being seen together at a Miguel show in L.A., Robert and Suki both attended TIFF at the same time:

The couple is spotted leaving a Christmas party at 5 a.m. in photos published by the good ol’ Daily Mail.

At this point, Robert and Suki are getting papped a ton and are seen enjoying a stroll through London in photos published by People.

Robert is interviewed by the Sunday Times and makes it clear that he will not be talking about Suki:

“Do I have to [talk about her]? If you let people in, it devalues what love is,” he says. “If a stranger on the street asked you about your relationship, you’d think it extremely rude. If you put up a wall, it ends up better...I can’t understand how someone can walk down the street holding hands, and it’s the same as when I do it and a hundred people are taking your photo.”

He concludes with, “The line between when you’re performing and when you’re not will eventually get washed away and you’ll go completely mad.” Fair!!

Robert and Suki get papped while celebrating his birthday in photos published by People. In other words, they are Still! Going! Strong!

The pair is spotted on a double date with, um, Taylor Swift and Joe Alwyn (?!?!?!) at San Vicente Bungalows in Hollywood, where a source tells Us Weekly they “were laughing and talking” all night and “were at a table, just the four of them.” Oh, and apparently, “Rob and Taylor were across from each other,” in case you were curious about the seating arrangement for whatever reason.

Robert and Suki make a suuuper-rare public appearance together at a Dior dinner during Paris Fashion Week.

And naturally, engagement rumors are sparked thanks to that ring ^^^ on Suki’s finger.

While chatting with GQ, Robert lets it slip that he’s staying “with his girlfriend” in a London apartment during quarantine.

Suki and Robert are spotted making out on a bench, as ya do!

A source tells E! News that Robert and Suki have “definitely discussed” getting engaged but that “Suki is not pressuring him and they are both very committed to each other.”

Fans are convinced there’s a picture of Robert and Suki kissing in the background of this solo Instagram she posts:

In a since-deleted tweet, Suki criticizes the Gossip Girl reboot for using this line: “He’s R-Patz and you’re Suki Nobody.”

“Another day to be reminded that women can also be the patriarchy. Seeing critiques of the patriarchy and sexism, then I get name-checked as somebody’s nobody girlfriend,” she says at the time. “Make it make sense.”

Robert once again refers to Suki as his girlfriend in a GQ interview, saying she couldn’t stop telling a man who had come to fix the boiler about his Batman role. “I’m sitting there facing the other direction, and my girlfriend just keeps continuing the conversation with him,” he recalls. “And I’m looking at her like, ‘Shut the fuck up. Why are you doing this to me?’ She was very entertaining. Just talking to an obsessive fan.”

He also talks about Suki’s reaction to the movie during an interview with Jimmy Kimmel, saying, “It was really her reaction that kind of changed the entire thing because I’m pretty sure she’s not normally into watching superhero movies. And just seeing that it was capturing her attention the entire time and then she held my hand and touched it [to her face] and I could feel a little tear. And I was like, ‘No way!’”

After more than four years of coupledom, Robert and Suki (*fiiiiinally*) make their red carpet debut at the Dior Men Fall 2023 show in Giza, Egypt, where they are the epitome of Fashion Goals™️ (she wears a mauve dress, he wears a silver-toned jacket over a v chill turtleneck):

In addition to making their ’ship red carpet official, Robert and Suki pose for several loved-up pics at the event.

Suki makes a rare comment about Robert during an interview with The Times, saying, “I’m shocked that I’m so happy with someone for nearly five years. I had two days off the other day and it was, like, no question I’m going back to see him. Then he’ll come out for a couple of days.”

She also says, “Rob definitely isn’t getting advice from me about acting, but of course I’ll try and get him to help me with an audition before he falls asleep on the sofa.” Oh, and elsewhere in the interview, the model *also* muses on how they both grew up in Southwest London and ended up together after having such similar journeys in the entertainment industry: “We always say that I never ever would have thought I’d go out with a boy from Barnes and he didn’t think he’d go out with a girl from Chiswick.”

Suki and Robert make their Met Gala debut!

These two step out at the GO Campaign’s annual GO Gala in Los Angeles—a pretty rare appearance!

Suki confirms she's pregnant while on stage at the Corona Capital Festival in Mexico and gushes, “I’m extra sparkly today because I thought it might distract you from something else that’s going on. I'm not sure if it’s working.”

Robert and Suki spark engagement rumors yet again in pics published by TMZ of the couple walking around London and the model specifically wearing a ring on *that* finger.

But it doesn’t take long for fans to find out the truth because, not long after, a source F-I-N-A-L-L-Y confirms that the duo is engaged! “They are engaged. They both want to be married. It’s important for them,” the insider dishes to People at the time.

The celebs officially welcome their first baby!!! And according to pics published by the Daily Mail, they’re already comfortable being out and about and walking their baby in a stroller (which btw is pink 🥺).

Suki posts the first photo of her newborn baby to Instagram, writing, “Welcome to the world angel ❤️.”

That same month, the new parents show up to Coachella!

And Suki confirms on stage that they welcomed a baby girl:

During an interview with British Vogue for the outlet’s August cover, Suki reveals that she and Robert met six years ago at a game night in L.A. and notes that the activity is “one of the things people do here to congregate.”

“I was sure that I’d met him a long time ago, but he didn’t think that we had,” she begins before detailing the events of the night.

On the docket was Werewolf (“think the The Traitors, and if you haven’t seen that, then wink murder”). “It was very, very intense...There were lots of ‘big’ characters, real heavy hitters...Al Pacino was there. Javier [Bardem] and Penélope [Cruz] were there…and, you know, everyone was really acting," she adds.

Except for Suki and Rob, obvi, who found themselves drawn to each other that night. “I think we both sort of have the same slight uncomfortable-ness,” the model reveals before adding that she and her now-fiancé “started giggling at the absurdity of the whole thing.”

The two didn't end up exchanging numbers at the get-together; however, they *did* run into each other nearly a year later. And the rest is history, people!

Oh, and during another part of the interview, Suki confesses that she and Rob fully planned on having their baby: “No, we really planned it...One day we looked at each other and said, ‘Well, this is as ready as we’re going to be.’” “I was like, ‘What can make more chaos?’” she hilariously adds.

Rob and Suki attend the Veuve Clicquot Polo Classic—making their first red carpet appearance as a couple since the birth of their daughter—and ofc, they look very, very chic.

Rob gives his own take on the night he and Suki met—where, apparently, he and Suki didn’t take Werewolf as seriously as the other celeb guests would have liked. “She was sitting opposite me,” Rob says of Suki during an interview with The New York Times before confirming that Javier Bardem, Penélope Cruz, and Al Pacino were indeed all present.

He continues, “Suki and I kept making each other laugh, to the point where someone told us we weren’t taking the game seriously enough. That was a very, very sweet moment.”

A tipster writes in to gossip site DeuxMoi, claiming Rob and Suki tied the knot in the Caribbean on New Year’s Eve.

“Robert Pattinson and Suki Waterhouse got married at NYE at the Caribbean resort I work at!!!” the source claims. “They were so cute and sweet, their baby was there in a gorgeous little bridesmaids dress.”

The insider adds that “their family was there as well” and it was “such a cute wedding”—plus, Rob and Suki were reportedly “so nice.”

Soon after, Sharon Stone hosts the American Heart Association Red Dress Collection Concert, where she introduces Suki to the stage to perform and mentions that Suki gave up time with her “10-month-old baby” and her “husband” to be there, per In Touch—and apparently, Suki didn’t correct her.

Rob and Suki hit the red carpet for the Die My Love premiere and look so, so chic.

A chic night out at the Vanity Fair Oscars after-party…

…followed by date night at the L.A. premiere of The Drama! I’m obsesssssed.

Oh! And during an interview with Extra, Rob and Zendaya are asked about their “biggest secret.” Zendaya playfully teases that there’s “nothing that comes to mind,” while Rob says his big secret is “the same one that you have” while grinning at Zendaya.

Robert and Suki roll up to the Venice International Film Festival looking 10/10.

After almost two years of speculation, Suki FINALLY addresses those marriage rumors, telling the Sunday Times that she and Robert are not, in fact, husband and wife...at least, not yet.

“It would either be all or nothing,” she says of their future wedding. “If we were going to go for it we’d have a massive party, blow it out.”

Of her relationship, she also gushes, “It is almost a decade and that is a beautiful thing. It’s a thing that I go, ‘I can’t believe I have this in my life and I can’t believe that I’m so happy.’”

#6871. The Best Knee-Length Skirts Worth Adding to Your Closet

From polished pencils to effortless denim, these are the skirts to shop now.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

There’s a reason the knee-length skirt has outlasted every trend cycle thrown at it. Sitting right at the knee, it’s not too short or too long, and it flatters everyone. This is the hemline of quiet confidence. It fits nearly every body type, plays well with flats, boots, and heels alike, and lets the fabric determine the overall vibes.

A structured A-line in wool crepe means business. A tailored pencil in a silky satin feels sexy and feminine without trying too hard. And the humble denim knee-length? Underrated, effortless, and endlessly styleable.

Ahead, we’ve rounded up the knee-length skirts worth your money, from investment-worthy classics to budget-friendly finds that look far pricier than they are.

Structure and movement define the overall silhouette of this pleated wool-blend skirt. It works just as well at the office as it does on the weekend, and the chocolate brown shade is a rich alternative to black. Plus, we love the unexpected slit.

Blending sporty and casual influences, this gabardine skirt has a lightweight structure and endless styling possibilities. It’s a relaxed weekend option that steps away from the usual shorts or joggers.

Silk and satin skirts continue to dominate the skirt category. This one feels feminine and timeless, with a delicate lace trim that adds a soft, romantic flair. Pair it with chic ballet pumps or tall brown boots for an autumnal twist.

The amount of wear you’ll get from a classic black silhouette at such an affordable price makes this knee-length style a must-have. Between the style, color, and fabric, you already know it has endless styling possibilities.

This silk tweed skirt pairs classic tailoring with a textured, luxe finish that feels heirloom-worthy from the first wear. Dress it up with a crisp blouse or down with a knit and loafers.

This misty gray knee-length skirt pairs a retro-inspired shape with a raw hem for an effortlessly modern edge. Style it with a fitted tee and sneakers for everyday ease, or a tucked-in blouse and ankle boots when you want a little more polish.

This panel-pleated wool-blend skirt channels a schoolgirl spirit with a polished, modern twist. Pair it with knee-high socks and loafers for a nostalgic nod, or a fitted knit and cap-toe block heels for an elevated everyday look.

This denim circle skirt hits right at the knee with a swingy, playful shape and a well-loved texture that only gets better with wear. It’s filled with rich character you’ll reach for season after season.

#6865. Stephen Graham’s Wife Would Like Him to Stop Following Strangers (Literally)

The Adolescence star plays an alluring serial killer in Nocturne. But his own fixation on studying people often leads to awkward encounters. “It was a lot easier when my face wasn’t as recognizable as it is now,” the actor tells Esquire.

Stephen Graham can’t help himself. The Emmy-winning actor doesn’t mean any harm. He just gets fixated on someone, and then...

“I watch people,” he says. “I used to watch people in train stations. Watch walks. And watch how they behave. Watch their mannerisms.” He thinks of this as ongoing performer training. “I’ll store it for later in a Rolodex in me mind,” Graham says in his lilting Liverpool accent, tapping his temple. “You absorb as much as you can. Then you just allow these things to come out.”

Graham plays a charismatic serial killer in the new Apple TV series Nocturne, and while he obviously doesn’t have the character’s murderous tendencies, the two do share a fascination with silently studying those around them. “It was a lot easier when my face wasn’t as recognizable as it is now,” the 53-year-old actor adds.

He became a familiar face in his role as Al Capone on Boardwalk Empire. Then with his scene-stealing performances in Martin Scorsese’s The Irishman and as Bruce Springsteen’s volatile father in Deliver Me from Nowhere. Winning Emmys, BAFTAs, and a Golden Globe for his work in front of and behind the camera on the harrowing crime drama Adolescence made it much harder to study people without being noticed. “I still do it now,” he says, before his wife cuts in.

“You get caught a lot now,” she corrects him.

Graham shrugs. “Now I get caught a lot,” he agrees.

It’s a sunny afternoon in the English Midlands, which is where Graham and his wife, Hannah Walters, keep their home. He’s speaking to Esquire via Zoom, his face covered with a scruffy beard and his hair lightly mussed. He’s wearing a bright-blue Teamsters Memorial Picnic T-shirt and a black-rimmed pair of glasses. The actor could pass as either a pub bouncer or a college professor.

“I’m at home, in the kitchen. We’re being very English,” he says. “My wife is making a cottage pie.” He spins the laptop around to face her. “Say hello, darling.”

Walters is also an actor, best known for the film and TV series Boiling Point, and she was a producer with her husband on Adolescence, costarring as the schoolteacher Mrs. Bailey. But she’s not feeling camera ready at the moment, stirring a steaming saucepan full of minced meat as loose locks of blond hair frame her face. “How very dare you…?” she says to Graham in her most haughty dowager voice before waving cheerfully.

She wasn’t officially involved in the making of Nocturne, but Walters was crucial in helping her husband wrap his head around the character of Jurek Walter, a serial killer whose crimes inexplicably continue even though he is locked up in a criminal asylum. He’s a soft-spoken manipulator, a master of analyzing behavior, and he’s able to wheedle into the minds of virtually anyone he encounters. Liev Schreiber plays a lawman on the outside who’s trying to figure out how Walter continues to puppeteer disappearances, and Zazie Beetz is an FBI agent who goes undercover in the detention facility to get close to him, at great personal risk. “And she was like, ‘Well, okay…’ ” The instruction began. “My research was watching crime documentaries with my wife.”

What did she say makes a good killer? “What makes a good killer?” he repeats to the cottage-pie baker.

She laughs off camera. “I don’t think anybody’s a good killer, are they? That’s an ironic statement,” she says. “But what makes a successful serial killer? I think intelligence. There’s a lot of serial killers that aren’t intelligent, but they get found out very quickly.”

“Highly intelligent. Pragmatic,” he says.

“We watched a lot of different people and just nicked little bits from each one,” Graham says. “Then your job as an actor is to try and create somebody who is believable, who is three-dimensional. Looking at the script, I saw the manipulation that Jurek is capable of and how he is a chameleon and he’ll adapt to each situation. So he reads the situation first and plays what he thinks it is that you want.”

That was about getting into Walter’s head. The second part of his prep was getting the right body. “I was getting myself in shape physically, where I looked like I could kill someone with my bare hands,” Graham says, holding his mitts out in front of him. “That was also one of my aims. Then you just build the character together with the director and with the writers.”

Showrunner John Hlavin (The Shield) says a lot of the work was already done by the time Graham arrived on the Pittsburgh set. “I mean, I’m not going to tell Stephen Graham how to do it. You know what I mean?” Hlavin says. “You’re dealing with a top line of professional athlete.” His contribution was making sure that Jurek Walter remained distinct from other infamous screen sociopaths, like Hannibal Lecter, who overpowers people with his erudition and mind games. Jurek Walter wins them over with vulnerability and sympathy.

Hlavin was struck by how quickly Graham could flip into character. “You’ve met him. He really likes to play. He’s very funny. He’s empathetic and personable. He sometimes would come to set singing, and it would echo around,” Hlavin says. “Then he snaps in. I would rehearse with him, we could be going over lines or making adjustments, and he would drop into character and I would be terrified. He’d go from friendly Stephen, chatting about the Steelers game he was invited to, to Jurek Walter.”

Graham gets physically animated when he talks about playing the role, like a little kid opening up a gift box that has something very dangerous inside. While his characters all have different mannerisms and voices, they often share a common trait: intimidation.

Maybe it’s his stocky, bruiser build that gets him cast in these roles, or maybe the cheerful Liverpudlian is just especially good at suppressing his own upbeat nature. He admits to getting a little overeager, especially when it comes to studying people in the wild.

That’s why it’s ironic that his wife, who was so helpful in guiding him to find the character of Jurek Walter, is so determined to hold him back from getting too close to passersby who intrigue him. “We were in London about two weeks ago, and some fella just started walking,” Graham says, rolling his shoulders back and forth. Imagine a person who moves like a too-tall stack of dishes.

“We both looked at him and she grabbed me by the arm and went, ‘Don’t,’ ” Graham says. “Because I’ll follow his walk, I’ll do his walk. I went, ‘But it’s a great walk!’ And she went, ‘Don’t you dare. Don’t you dare.’ I was like, ‘Arrgh … okay.’ ”

Not only is it awkward when the person spots him, but now that he’s renowned around the world, there are public-relations concerns to think about. His wife speaks up from across the kitchen: “Stephen Graham Followed Me into the London Underground—Headline!”“We’re influenced by so many different things,” Graham says. “Do you know what I mean?”

So if you find yourself on the street someday, being watched by someone who looks like the Emmy winner, don’t be alarmed. But you might see your distinctive walk end up onscreen someday.

#6861. All About Joaquim Valente, Gisele Bündchen’s Husband and Father of Her 3rd Child

Supermodel Gisele Bündchen has entered a new era of her relationship with professional jiu-jitsu trainer Joaquim Valente. On Feb. 5, 2025, TMZ broke the news that the couple recently welcomed their first child. A day later, a source told People they had a baby boy.

Bündchen started dating Valente in June 2023, following her 2022 divorce from Tom Brady. Valente and Bündchen were friends before becoming romantically involved.

In March 2024, Bündchen confirmed that she and Valente were together. In an interview with The New York Times, she said, “This is the first time I am seeing someone that was a friend of mine first. It’s very different. It is very honest, and it’s very transparent.”

It was announced that they had married in December 2025.

So how did they meet...and how did their love story later begin? Ahead, a timeline of their relationship and everything you need to know about Valente.

According to People, Bündchen and Valente were together for over half a year before news of their relationship broke in February 2024.

“They’ve been dating since June. They’re taking it slow,” a source revealed. “They started out as great friends first. She’s very private about this and wanted to keep it quiet while they got to know each other.”

“They started out as friends,” a second insider told People. “He was a huge comfort for Gisele while she went through her divorce. Their friendship turned romantic after the divorce.”

“Joaquim is a great guy,” they added. “He is down to earth, kind, and inspiring. Gisele was hesitant about dating after the divorce. It’s been very natural for her to date Joaquim.”

Bündchen also “loves that he is Brazilian” like her, the source furthered. “They come from similar backgrounds. They both left Brazil very young. They have both created amazing lives for themselves in the U.S. They both love Miami, but also enjoy traveling. They both enjoy healthy living. Gisele’s in a great space. She is happy and very much enjoying life. Joaquim is perfect for her.”

Bündchen and Valente had happy news in late October. A source told People in a statement that Bündchen is pregnant with their first child together and her third. The source said, “Gisele and Joaquim are happy for this new chapter in their life, and they’re looking forward to creating a peaceful and loving environment for the whole family.”

A few days later, on November 4, People shared comments from a source saying Bündchen is “excited to embrace this new chapter openly.”

The insider added that the model has “received many positive messages and congratulations,” but “wanted to keep it private for as long as she could, doing so was starting to be a challenge.”

She “feels good” and is continuing “Pilates and other exercises that will help with birth too,” the source added. Bündchen has “always taken amazing care of herself,” and “eats healthy and meditates as well.”

The baby’s sex and birth date were kept private, but TMZ reported on February 5 that Bündchen “recently” gave birth to her and Valente’s first child. She and the baby are “healthy,” the outlet was told, and Bündchen is “super happy.”

On February 6, a source told People that they had a boy to whom they gave the middle name River. All of Bündchen’s children have water-themed nicknames.

Before they started dating, they were good friends who were photographed vacationing together multiple times.

Bündchen and Valente were seen going for a jog in Costa Rica in January 2023. A source shut down dating rumors then, saying “he’s been her kids’ jiu-jitsu teacher for years. There’s no truth to the rumors that they’re dating.”

On Nov. 16, 2022, photographs of Bündchen and Valente on a trip to Costa Rica came out, this time with her children—and his jiu-jitsu students—Benjamin Rein, then 13, and Vivian Lake, then 10.

Bündchen can thank her preteen son for her relationship with Valente. He is one-third of the Valente Brothers, and they are co-founders of a jiu-jitsu academy in Miami. In 2021, Bündchen signed her son up for classes at the martial arts school. After taking him to his first class and speaking with Valente, she decided to enroll herself.

On Feb. 23, 2023, Bündchen posted an Instagram Reel of her sparring in the Valente Brothers’ studio.

“I believe the more tools we have in our toolbox the better. I feel stronger, more confidence and empowered since I started practicing self-defense. I feel it’s an important skill for all, but specially for us women. Thank you @ValenteBrothers for being awesome teachers and for making training so much fun. I am looking forward to keep improving. Let’s go! 🤛🏼,” she wrote in the caption.

Valente has been studying jiu-jitsu since he was two years old.

In Bündchen’s March 2024 interview with the New York Times, she addressed rumors that she was seeing Valente before her divorce from Tom Brady, saying, “That is a lie.”

They have to deal with their family. Of course for me, it just happens to be a little bit amplified.”

If you were hoping to get some Insta-stalking in, you’ll be disappointed. Valente does have an Instagram account, but he has it set to private currently.

#6860. Your Next Signature Scent Should Be Coffee-Inspired

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

These 11 colognes will inspire you to switch up your rotation.

I am not a coffee drinker. Never have been. The taste is too bitter for my liking, and that much caffeine will just keep me up all night, no matter what time of day I drink it. And coffee breath . . . But the smell of an actual cup of coffee? It’s divine. Rich. Sophisticated. And perfect for personal fragrance.

Perfumers include coffee notes in fragrance by using gourmand accords made from roasted coffee beans, espresso extracts, or aromatic molecules that mimic those smells. If you read through the scent notes on your favorite coffee cologne, you might notice something called “Coffee CO2 extract”—that’s a concentrated aromatic and bioactive oil created by passing supercritical carbon dioxide through roasted coffee beans. Pretty cool, right?

But coffee notes don’t always smell the same. Roasted coffee beans or espresso can smell dark and dry with a slightly bitter and earthy quality, whereas sweeter coffee notes like mocha and latte scents can be warm, milky, and rich. It also, of course, depends on what other notes are mixed in, from woody and earthy scents to sweet, gourmand ones. To give you an idea of the range, we rounded up the best coffee-scented colognes out there.

You’ll see plenty of pricey bottles on this list. We will get cerebral about perfume—men’s and women’s is a dumb distinction for scent. We will try to push you toward something interesting. But before all that, we have to pay respects. You might call Polo Red, first released in 2013, a garden-variety mall fragrance, something catering to the everyman. That is not our opinion. For us, considering the price point, it’s a modern classic.

Polo Red is a bit more interesting than your typical guy fragrance. It blends fresh, fruity notes of grapefruit, lemon, and cranberry with warm, rich notes of redwood, amber, and coffee. Add in earthy sage and woods and you get a really interesting combination that goes from bright and airy to deep and sensual thanks to the base notes of coffee, which blend beautifully with the warm woods and amber.

As always, Loewe delivers on its luxury fragrance—even the bottle looks like something you’d find in a museum. Like Polo Red, Loewe’s Wild Coffee brings together fruity notes and coffee, but instead of relying on light citrus fruits, it has notes of ripe mango, bitter orange, guava, and passion fruit, which provide a juicy, tropical backdrop for the rich coffee, amber, and tonka bean to come through.

It’s ridiculous how good Bois Corsé is. It’s a simple fragrance that captures “the textured outer layer of a tree.” Meaning, bark.

The notes are coffee, sandalwood, and tonka bean. The coffee is deep and chocolatey. The sandalwood gives it an earthiness, a bit of air and smoke. The tonka bean grounds it all in a warm, user-friendly base. It’s a masterclass, really. Straight down the middle and sure to get compliments

The coffee note is not as pronounced here, as this is a busy fragrance. You get Davana on top—an herb from the southern portion of the Indian subcontinent, which in perfume form presents green and herby but also a bit boozy, like a nice cordial. Like in most of these other scents, there’s a warm tonka note that pairs with the coffee. The oud is not a boring smoky presentation, but a pretty earthy one.

The overall experience is, as the name might suggest, a busy street corner in a tropical climate. There’s a lot going on, but it’s all working well together—a really captivating scent.

In September, DedCool launched its new viral fragrance, Mocha Magic. It’s a gourmand through and through, but it creates some intrigue by contrasting bittersweet notes of espresso, mocha, and bourbon with more classically sweet notes of cream, biscotti, toffee, and vanilla. In a word: Mmmm.

Speaking of gourmands, Maison Margiela’s Replica collection includes the perfect sweet and spicy take on coffee. It emphasizes the scents of warm coffee, milk mousse, and sweet vanilla at its base, but before we get there, it’s actually quite fresh and uplifting thanks to lemon, lavender, and spearmint. So you get a bit of citrus and herbal freshness before leaning into the rich gourmand of it all.

Dolce & Gabbana’s Devotion for Men will surely turn you on to coffee scents. Warm, sensual, and woody, it highlights herbal lavender, dark coffee, and smooth woods, with an earthy backdrop of oakmoss. It’s a solid, elegant pick for a nice dinner or an evening out.

Now here’s a combination you probably weren’t expecting: refined rose and spicy coffee. As it turns out, light florals go really nicely with black coffee, and it’s a cool contrast you really don’t find anywhere else. And, in classic Tom Ford fashion, there’s a bit of smoky frankincense at the base that gives the fragrance a dark and sexy twist.

Coffee and woods make sense together, and when notes of roasted coffee beans are combined with a variety of different woods, it makes for something smooth and sophisticated. That’s what Roja does here. The nutty side of coffee is brought out by notes of cinnamon, pistachio, and caramelized nuts, while cedarwood, sandalwood, oud, and dry woods emphasize its deeper, drier side.

Inspired by the Italian dessert affogato—hot espresso poured over vanilla ice cream—Mancera’s Amore Caffe blends delicious gourmand notes of amaretto liqueur, vanilla ice cream, speculoos, and brown sugar with black coffee to give it that addictive bittersweet quality.

Sweet, spicy, and woody, Kilian’s Intoxicated is another beloved coffee scent, though it’s definitely a strong one. So if you want something bold, this is a good pick. The unique mix of dark mocha coffee with green cardamom, cinnamon, nutmeg, and vetiver gives it a rich, sexy, and spicy intrigue.

#6857. Martha Stewart’s Halloween Costume Controversy Continues After Several Apologies

Even after multiple apologies, the controversy is still following her.

Halloween has come and gone, but Martha Stewart is still facing the horrors. The famous homemaker has issued several apologies after her controversial costume raised eyebrows.

In case you somehow missed her since-deleted post, Martha seemingly dressed as an unhoused person for the holiday. She wore blue jeans, a torn-up flannel and a cardigan, and a beanie with fake dirt on her face as she held up a handwritten sign that read, “I am hungry. Help me!!”

People were equally outraged and confused by her costume choice, questioning the decision altogether.

“How do you even think this is a good halloween costume in the first place…” one person wrote, while another added, “Rich celebrities mocking poor people and their struggles is so dystopian...this is disgusting.”

Others called Martha out for being “out of touch” and “detached from reality,” as a recent report from the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development reveals that homelessness has increased by 27 percent between 2013 and 2025.

Upon receiving backlash for the costume, the 85-year-old multi-hyphenate released a statement to Page Six after deleting her photo.

“I am distressed that my Halloween costume yesterday, which was intended to bring more awareness to the very serious subject of homelessness, has offended people, which was never my intention,” she told the outlet on Sunday, November 1.

“Homelessness continues to remain one of America’s greatest challenges and ongoing crises, something that I care deeply about and I have spent a significant amount of time and effort supporting charities that combat this ongoing social epidemic,” she continued, adding that she is “committed to continuing to make a difference.”

If you thought the drama ended there, spoiler alert: It didn’t. After Just Jared posted on Instagram about Martha’s costume, she dropped a comment, writing, “My costume was designed to bring the homeless and the hungry to everyone's mind and boy it certainly did!!! But not in the correct way, because I did not explain it and I am so sorry I care and I support causes who care . Do all of you???”

Shortly after she commented on the post, actor, model, and activist Indya Moore shared a lengthy reply explaining why the costume was problematic.

“We all make mistakes. I certainly do. I try my best to correct them, not just with explanations but with more active support around the actual issue I may be contributing to. But this is a really tough one for me,” Indya wrote in part.

“It also seems like you apologizing for not writing a caption and our subsequent failure to understand the outfit—not because you wore it,” she later continued before calling Martha out further and sharing organizations to help the cause.

“If you were serious about poverty, you’d use your platform to back policies that tax the rich, put free food in front of people who are hungry and homeless, and provide free healthcare and education,” Indya added.

After Indya’s comment, Martha shared a series of Halloween-related content, including a “gay Halloween” post referencing a time she handed water to a child in New York City and a wholesome jack-o’-lantern pic. She then issued another Notes App apology for her costume.

“It’s hard to admit when you’ve really screwed up,” she wrote, in part. “My earlier statement didn’t go far enough. I know many people are suffering economically. Wearing a costume like I did was insensitive and not funny. I am very sorry.”

That’s all for now, but we’ll be back with more as we have it. (You know, in case there’s another jump scare in this mess.)

If you or someone you know is unhoused or are at risk of losing housing, call 2-1-1 to connect with local resources and support, including emergency shelter, housing assistance, and other essential services, or visit 211.org to find help in your area. Calls are free and confidential.

#6855. 6 Safety Razors That’ll Make You Swear Off Multiblade and Electric Shaving

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

One blade gives you a smoother shave and is better for your skin.

If you shave your face with any sort of frequency (daily, every other day, weekly), chances are you use a multiblade cartridge razor—a Gillette razor is what smart money would bet on. It’s the most common option for a reason. Cartridge razors are cheap, readily available, and easy to pick up new blades for (which frequently aren’t as cheap). But if you’ve often found yourself suffering from razor bumps, ingrown hairs, or razor burn—all issues that come readily to those with coarse or thick hair—you might need a course correction. That is because multiblade razors tend to cut too close. Or, well ... it’s not that they cut too close; they cut too much. By the time three to five blades track over a hair follicle, it’s underneath the outer layer of the skin. At that point, it’s simple mathematics that some of these hairs will not grow back perfectly straight out of your skin. They get stuck underneath your epidermis and cause those big, fat ingrown pimples.

Out in the world of shaving, there are single-blade razors, safety razors, multiblade cartridge razors, hell, even electric razors. In the past decade or so, traditional single-blade razors, aka safety razors, have enjoyed a bit of a renaissance. These blades are particularly good for men with sensitive skin or that thick, coarse hair. Safety razors, since they have only one blade, are able to shave close to the skin without going overboard. That comes with some risk. It is easier to cut yourself with, since the blade is sharp and unprotected by some of the features—like moisturizing bars—that you find in cartridge razors. There’s also a pretty steep learning curve associated with them, particularly if you come from the cartridge-razor world.

For those who put in the work and get the hang of it, many say a safety razor gives a far superior shave. And they do save you money in the long run. What else are you waiting for? Oh, for me to tell you what the best ones are. Easy enough. For my money, these six are the best safety razors you can buy.

Some might say Bevel was responsible for the safety-razor renaissance of the past decade with the release of this razor (one of its very first products). The thought behind this design, and the reason many people prefer safety razors, is that they don’t cut as close to the skin as multiblade razors, which helps prevent razor bumps and ingrown hairs. That’s a great thing for men with coarse and/or curly hair, which tends to form ingrowns more easily—and it’s also great for anyone with sensitive skin.

This handy razor is on the weighty side at over four ounces, which can be a good thing for a safety razor, and the sleek design is easy to understand and use. The double-edged blades are easy to replace using the screw top. Whether you’re a safety-razor beginner or expert, this is always going to be a good pick.

If you’re new to safety razors, investing in an expensive one might not be your top priority. This budget-friendly razor is all you need in order to get the hang of safety-razor shaving. It has a traditional screw-top design that uses the same double-edged blades as some of the more expensive handles on this list. The handle has a good heft to it that, combined with an easy-to-grip handle, helps you get used to shaving at a 30-degree angle. The closed-comb design is ideal for beginners and pros alike and delivers a smooth, close shave with minimal risk of nicks and cuts. If you’re a beginner, it’s a great option to learn with. If you’re a pro, it’s a great travel option so you can leave your pricier tool at home.

If you’re starting shaving with a safety razor from scratch, there are a few things to know. The two key elements are the weight of the razor and the angle of the blade. You want a handle that is hefty and feels heavy in your hand, not so light that you’re tempted to move it too quickly over the surface of your skin. The right weight lets the blade do most of the work and allows you to shave your hair with minimal strokes and less risk of nicks. The blade angle should always be about 30 degrees, not perpendicular to your skin, so you usually want to hold the razor at a slight angle.

This well-designed razor from pubic grooming giants Manscaped takes both of those into account. The weight is perfectly hefty, and the blade is actually already designed to sit at a 30 degree angle, so you don’t need to worry as much about how you’re holding it. Both of those things make this razor a perfect jumping off point for those who are coming from the multiblade side.

Safety razors are good for a lot of things, but if you’ve ever used one to try to tackle a few days’ worth of stubble, you know it’s not always great at powering through a lot of growth—at least not with the risk of nicking or cutting yourself going up exponentially. That’s where this safety razor shines. There are six different adjustable settings that subtly widen the space between the razor and the comb, allowing for thicker and coarser hair to be shaved more easily. If you shave every day, you’ll likely want to keep it on the lowest (zero gap) setting, which is basically your classic safety-razor setting, but if you tend to go a few days between shaves, change the setting to allow you to shave your stubble more easily with fewer strokes.

Sometimes you gotta go with a classic. Merkur has been making razors in Germany for over 125 years, and you can’t argue with that pedigree. This traditional safety razor has a traditional screw-top design and a weighted head that allows you to get a silky-smooth shave pretty much immediately. There aren’t any newfangled features in this thing, but that’s kind of the point. The nonslip coating and the perfectly weighted head, as well as the closed-comb design, make this the ideal short handle traditional razor.

Up until this point, I’ve been talking about closed comb razors. The difference, as you can see, is the exposed teeth of the blade on this style, making it sharper but also more of a risky proposition. Knowing your safety razor basics is a must, but if you have the technique down, this is a way to tackle longer or coarser beards.

Don’t get me wrong, this is an expert move. For those of us who grow facial hair faster and can never get a shave as close as we want before it starts growing back, the open comb razor is one way to achieve this. The cleanest shave of your life won’t just feel great, many say it will make your hair grow back slower.

Whether or not you’re experienced with safety razors, there are a few things to take into account when choosing the right one for you.

Weight: You’ll notice that most safety razors are way heavier than the drugstore cartridge razors you may be used to, and it’s not just because they’re made of metal. That’s by design. Safety razors tend to concentrate their weight in the head, which allows them to use gravity to give you a smooth shave without applying too much pressure (which can lead to nicks and cuts). Not all safety razors are the same weight, though. Ultimately you want to find a razor that is heavy enough to give you a good, close shave but not so heavy that your hand gets tired halfway through the process.

Blades: Most safety razors will use a traditional double-edged blade. This allows you to use both sides of the blade to get a little more usage out of it. Some razors will use a single-edged blade, usually called an injector blade, which is often thicker and more durable than its double-edged counterparts. Double-edged blades are preferable, but whichever you choose, make sure refills are readily available, as you’ll need to change the blade every few uses. (Most safety razors, especially those that use double-edged blades, can be refilled with any brand of blade.)

Razor Head Type: Speaking of changing the blades, safety razors typically come with two types of head. The most common is screw-off, where you unscrew the head of the razor in order to change the blade. The downfall of these is that while they’re easy in theory, it means the head comes in multiple parts that must be unscrewed and then screwed back together, making for a somewhat complicated experience, especially for beginners. The other kind, butterfly, pops up in the middle and allows you to drop in the blade.

Open Comb vs. Closed Comb: In this case, “comb” refers to how much distance there is between the razor head and the blade itself. The more open the comb, the more aggressive the shave. Closed comb razors are especially ideal for beginners because they help cut down on the risk of nicks and cuts (as there is no space for skin to get caught). Open-comb razors are ideal for people with thicker beards or longer stubble, as they allow more hair to reach the blade at once, but they carry a higher risk for nicks and cuts.

Adjustable Settings: Some razors will allow you to adjust the settings to take it between a closed and an open comb. Usually this involves different inserts that push space between the blade and the razor head. While not always necessary for everyone, this is ideal if you like to allow time between shaves and sometimes shave with a few days’ worth of stubble or more.

#6854. This “Cool Girl” British Lighting Brand Is Sold on Amazon, and I Just Bought Its Cutest Lamp

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

When it comes to buying home decor, I follow a strict “bougie on a budget” philosophy. Sure, I love to fill my space with charming, elevated items, but I’m not exactly willing to spend a small fortune to outfit my rental apartment. So you can imagine my sheer surprise and delight when I learned you can buy a lamp from Pooky Lighting right on Amazon.

If you know, you know, but just in case you don’t? The British brand has been the cool girl’s secret to great lighting ever since it crossed the pond in 2024. Pooky might sell every fixture imaginable—and even have collaborations with Novogratz and Morris &. Co.—but its pièce de résistance is the portable, rechargeable lights.

A whimsical glow that goes wherever you go? Sold...well, if they weren’t so expensive. A quick scroll shows prices starting at $115 and easily going above $200. Not exactly conducive to my “bougie on a budget” mindset. When I stumbled upon two Pooky portable lights for $100 on Amazon, I thought it was a mistake or a very convincing knockoff. However, upon further inspection, these lights seem legit. Though both lights have the same lower price tag, they have two completely different looks. With a pleated shade and bulbous base, the Cecily lamp will pair nicely with midcentury-inspired or even contemporary spaces. If, like me, you are looking for something more aligned with Pooky’s English roots, might I suggest the Primrose style? The woven shade and scalloped details fit perfectly with my apartment’s traditional coastal vibe. Both options come in seven colorways, so you’re bound to find one that matches your interiors.

As soon as my lamp arrived, I charged the Genesis Cell light source, which can reportedly hold a charge for up to 17 hours, and immediately placed it on my kitchen counter. I always joke I’m allergic to harsh, overhead lighting, so I love how this fixture illuminates my culinary space without making the entire room obnoxiously, blindingly bright. And, since it's cordless, I can really take it anywhere. Next to my oven when I cook? Above the dishwasher when I’m only washing plates and glassware? Near the coffee machine to make my morning brew? All of the above, and then some.

But perhaps my favorite detail is the accompanying remote control, which has a timer setting. It’s all too easy to turn on a portable lamp, forget about it, and wind up with zero battery the next time you need a light. With this fixture, I can light my kitchen for 15, 30, or 60 minutes and have it magically turn off on its own. Now that’s a bright idea.
